# Synthesis-layer verify — git-diff before commit, decompose before flip

# TIER-3 port provenance only: ported from Z:/claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md HEAD. Parent CCC retains the n=9 dogfood chain and cycle-N evidence; it is not claude-sota design authority.

At the team-lead synthesis-pre-commit gate, verify every teammate ship-report claim against `git diff` (claimed edits) and `git status` (claimed new files) BEFORE trusting the report. For new code, additionally smoke-run or import-check it. The rule applies regardless of WHY ship-report drifted — recovery pattern is invariant under failure-mode taxonomy.

## Why

Ship-reports drift from git reality in BOTH directions OR fail to surface a finding entirely (HONEST-NON-FINDING — see §Reporting categories), and the failure-mode taxonomy keeps growing (agent flake, proxy restart, 429 retries, Windows loopback transients, model-side stalls, infrastructure deploys mid-cycle). The synthesis-layer recovery is the same single check across all of them: `git diff` + `git status` + smoke-import. Without this check, false-failure re-spawns waste 2-5 min each; false-success commits introduce silent no-ops into git history.

## How to apply (team-lead pre-commit checklist)

For every claimed file edit:
- Run `git diff <path>` and confirm the diff matches the report's described change (additions + deletions present, not empty, not inverted).

For every claimed new file:
- Run `git status --short` (or `ls -la <path>`) and confirm presence + non-zero size.

For every new code file:
- Smoke-run a minimal invocation — `python -c "import <module>"`, `<hook> --help`, or at minimum re-run the project's static-analysis baseline (`pyright`, `ruff`) on the new file. File existence ≠ file imports cleanly.

If teammate ship-report contradicts git state in EITHER direction:
- Do NOT commit blindly — investigate via `git reflog`, partial-write evidence, infrastructure event log, or active-task list before re-spawning or accepting.

Anti-pattern: trusting "wrote 200 LOC across 3 files" without running `git status` to count new/changed files first.

## SubagentStop transcript-mining (4th OVER/UNDER/HNF detection axis — iter-109 closure)

Beyond the 3 git-state-derived axes above (`git diff` claimed edits / `git status` claimed new files / smoke-import for new code), the **SubagentStop transcript-mining axis** surfaces evidence ORTHOGONAL to git verification — what the agent CONSIDERED but DIDN'T SHIP, tool-call sequences, prompt-cache hits — visible in the subagent transcript JSONL (`agent_transcript_path` per Anthropic SDK `SubagentStopHookInput` schema at `Z:/repos/deps/claude-agent-sdk-python/src/claude_agent_sdk/types.py:309-316 @ HEAD b512f256450dba8f0dd1399e485563b7deb9c534` [VERIFIED 2026-05-02 via Ship 38 Marker Decay refresh]).

**Operational form**: `.claude/hooks/scripts/subagent_transcript_mine.py` SubagentStop hook (Layer 3 async fail-open observer per `layered-gates-architecture.md §6`) appends to `.claude/state/subagent_transcripts.jsonl` — schema: `{ts, agent_id, agent_type, transcript_path, last_text[:2000], tool_count, tool_errors, parse_status}`. Sentinel discipline per Karpathy P1 lenient-mode: `parse_status` ∈ {ok / no_transcript_path / transcript_missing / parse_empty / parse_failed}; `-1` for tool_count/tool_errors when uncertain.

**Detection contracts**:
- **OVER (transcript-vs-report)**: agent's `verdict_one_line` claims `DONE: 5 fixes` but transcript shows `tool_count=2` — transcript truth refutes ship-report. Recovery: investigate via JSONL trace + revisit ship; same shape as `git diff` OVER.
- **UNDER (transcript surfaces unreported tool-errors)**: agent's report doesn't mention errors but transcript shows `tool_errors > 0` — agent encountered failures it suppressed. Recovery: surface the suppressed errors per cardinal-rule 7 (CLAUDE.md §Hard Rules, bullet "REPORT errors before routing around them"); pivot ship if errors load-bearing.
- **HONEST-NON-FINDING (transcript empty / parse failure)**: `parse_status != "ok"` — transcript missing or unparseable. Recovery: HNF disposition; do NOT re-spawn agent on the assumption transcript exists; treat axis as closed-without-finding.

**Caveat (per `agent-harness-fit-verification.md` Probe 5 mode-harness-shape)**: hook fires on SubagentStop; if SDK runtime ceiling (e.g., max_turns) terminates the session before SubagentStop fires, this hook has no event to mine. Verdicts using this axis MUST mark `[VERIFIED via .claude/state/subagent_transcripts.jsonl]` per `cross-model-consensus.md §Evidence marker discipline` MUST-USE convention codified iter-107.

**Sister-rule integration**:
- `.claude/rules/audit-action-loop.md §Hook telemetry contract` — same `agent_id`/`agent_type` propagation contract; this hook follows the SDK schema at top-level
- `.claude/rules/parallel-agent-wave.md §Cache-Aware Dispatch Pacing` — `tool_count` mining feeds cost-aware termination predicate per iter-108 codification
- `.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md §Stuck Detection` — `tool_errors > 0` mining feeds 3-failed-attempts STOP signal
- `.claude/rules/cross-model-consensus.md §Evidence marker discipline` — `[VERIFIED via path]` MUST-USE per iter-107 codification applies to JSONL cite

## Reporting categories — OVER, UNDER, HONEST-NON-FINDING

Ship-reports fall into three reporting categories at the synthesis layer, each with a distinct recovery pattern. The categories are exhaustive — every report fits one — and the recovery action differs by category.

| Category | Definition | Synthesis-layer response |
|---|---|---|
| **OVER** | Report claims more than git shows (claimed edit reverted; claimed file absent) | `git reflog` + infrastructure event log; investigate before re-spawn or commit |
| **UNDER** | Report claims less than git shows (claimed nothing, file landed; partial credit) | `git diff` to surface the orphan change; accept work + correct report → commit |
| **HONEST-NON-FINDING** | Report says "I probed for X, found nothing" — when the brief assumed X exists | Pivot ship — DROP / REDIRECT / amend brief. The HNF IS the deliverable; do NOT re-spawn the agent to "find X anyway" |

**Why HNF matters**: it's the highest-value report shape but easiest to mistake for "agent failed to ship." An agent that probes the brief's assumption and finds it wrong has saved a wrong-direction ship — that's a SUCCESS at the discipline layer, not a failure. The synthesis-layer recovery is to update the brief / drop the patch / redirect to the correct target — never to re-spawn under the same (now-refuted) assumption.

**HNF as cite-vs-concept sibling**: HNF reports are often the empirical refutation of a brief-level cite-vs-concept failure. Brief asserts X exists at file:line; agent probes; X doesn't exist. Brief's assumption was a CONCEPT (loose familiarity) not a CITE (verified file:line). HNF surfaces this gap. (Cite-vs-concept semantic-equivalence remains FORWARD-REF in claude-sota; the parent CCC narrative is TIER-3 provenance only until ported with TIER-1/TIER-2 backing.)

## Subclaim-type discriminator (UPSTREAM / OPERATIONAL / CATEGORY)

When applying within-cycle-revision-discipline to a freshness-audit candidate, classify the SUBCLAIM by type BEFORE allocating probe budget. Three categorical types; the type predicts probe yield.

| Subclaim type | Definition | Probe yield |
|---|---|---|
| **UPSTREAM-CLAIM** | About external project's state (README content, repo metadata, framing) — subject to upstream evolution between verdict-cycle and probe-cycle | HIGH refutation candidate — upstream may have reformed |
| **OPERATIONAL-CLAIM** | About OUR harness state (live MCP invocations, hook-fire counts, integration depth) — controlled by us, slow-moving | LOW refutation likelihood — state changes only when we change it |
| **CATEGORY-CLAIM** | About semantic mismatch between candidate and our use-case (RAG vs session-memory, batch vs streaming) — requires upstream pivot to flip | LOW refutation likelihood — categorical signals stable |

**Mixed types**: a single candidate's subclaim chain can include all three. CATEGORY-CLAIM ("API-redundant with X") + OPERATIONAL-CLAIM ("our X integration is live") can both apply to the same exclusion verdict.

**How to apply (pre-probe budget allocation)**:
1. Type the claim — UPSTREAM / OPERATIONAL / CATEGORY?
2. Estimate probe budget — UPSTREAM gets full 5-axis (WebFetch README + GitHub API + convergence-gate axes); OPERATIONAL gets spot-check via local grep; CATEGORY gets one-pass README scan
3. Honest classification — if uncertain, default to UPSTREAM-CLAIM (under-probing has higher false-PASS cost than over-probing)

**Distinguishes from §Reporting categories above**: §Reporting categories classifies REPORT shape (OVER/UNDER/HNF — about agent's output). §Subclaim-type discriminator classifies SOURCE shape (UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY — about what's being verified). Orthogonal axes — a UPSTREAM-CLAIM probe may produce HNF, OVER, or UNDER report.

## Output-form verification modifier (SHAPE-CLAIM)

After typing the subclaim by source as UPSTREAM / OPERATIONAL / CATEGORY, add **SHAPE-CLAIM** when the claim depends on **structured output that can be checked before deployment**: TypeScript workflow code, Pydantic / BaseModel payloads, Zod / Effect schemas, JSON Schema validated tool args, `PINS.json` entries, `settings.json` hook entries, `.mcp.json` server configs, MCP tool args, or hook JSONL payloads.

**SHAPE-CLAIM is a MODIFIER, not a source type.** It combines with each source class:
- `UPSTREAM-CLAIM + SHAPE-CLAIM`: PINS.json bumps citing upstream HEAD SHA + content-SHA (the bump is upstream-state; the content shape is checkable)
- `OPERATIONAL-CLAIM + SHAPE-CLAIM`: settings.json hook entries, .mcp.json server configs, hook JSONL payloads (the hook fires per harness state; the JSON shape validates)
- `CATEGORY-CLAIM + SHAPE-CLAIM`: agent frontmatter or skill SKILL.md (the frontmatter is structured + parseable; the categorical role is semantic)

**Probe rule**: run the strongest available compile/schema/validation check first. If it passes, downgrade only **shape-refutation budget**; still run semantic/runtime probes for side effects, credentials, provider limitations, type coercion, `any`/untyped fields, unsupported schema keywords, and producer/consumer boundary mismatches. **Compile-pass is NOT deploy-pass.**

**Convergence-gate evidence** (per `.claude/rules/convergence-gate.md`):
- **Axis 1** (≥3 T1 sources from distinct orgs) — **PASS**:
  1. **n8n MCP TypeScript-output** (`blog.n8n.io/n8n-mcp-server/` 2026-04-29 [VERIFIED 2026-05-02]) — "Generates code, not JSON ... must type-check and compile before anything touches your instance"
  2. **Effect Schema** (`effect.website/docs/schema/introduction/` [VERIFIED 2026-05-02]) — TypeScript `Schema<Type, Encoded, Requirements>` + `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` compile-time enforcement; "the type checker will catch any attempt to assign an invalid value"
  3. **Pydantic v2** (`pydantic.dev/docs/validation/latest/get-started/why/` [VERIFIED 2026-05-02]) — Python type hints as schema specification; `BaseModel` core primitive; dual-purpose IDE static analysis + runtime validation + JSON Schema generation
- **Axis 2** (≥2 named T2 practitioners with dated artifact) — **PARTIAL**: creator/maintainer endorsements (Samuel Colvin / Michael Arnaldi / n8n vendor representative) + official docs are close to but not equal to the dated-practitioner-artifact-citing-this-specific-pattern evidence shape required by convergence-gate Axis 2. Re-evaluate when an independent named-T2 dated artifact citing the specific compile-before-deploy pattern lands.
- **Axis 3** (≥3 months stability) — **PASS**: Pydantic v2 ~2.5y, Effect Schema ~5y; n8n MCP TypeScript-output substrate mature (the workflow-build feature is new but the TypeScript-as-schema substrate is not).

**Anti-patterns** (per codex T1 F-002 + Q7 prescription):
- **Free-form JSON when a schema/compiler exists** — n8n catches this; "code beats JSON" for LLM-emitted structured output
- **Compile-pass treated as deploy-pass** — Effect docs warn: `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` runtime mismatch is possible if the compiler flag is disabled
- **Type hints without runtime validation at untrusted boundaries** — Pydantic validators are runtime-separate; type hints alone don't validate user input
- **`any`, `dict[str, Any]`, permissive JSON Schema, missing `additionalProperties: false` treated as strong shape proof** — these are escape hatches that nullify the gate
- **Schema generated but not actually used at the producer/consumer boundary** — generation without enforcement is a documentation comment, not a gate
- **Provider-constrained schema accepted without post-parse validation against the original schema** — Anthropic structured-outputs simplifies schemas at the API boundary; post-parse validation against the original schema closes the gap

**Cite trail**: codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.88 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_ship6_shape_claim_OUT.txt` (Pattern A fix-forward integrated per `.claude/rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md`). 3 TIER-1 primary sources verified via WebFetch [VERIFIED 2026-05-02]; convergence-gate Axis 1 PASS strict, Axis 2 PARTIAL pending dated-T2 artifact, Axis 3 PASS.

## Pre-apply Mia rule on agent ship-prescriptions

Mechanics moved to `.claude/rules/mia-pre-apply.md` as the standalone owner rule.
This section is a router only: apply the owner rule before accepting agent `prescribed_edits` or concrete file:line gap claims.
Keep this heading for legacy §-anchor cites; do not duplicate mechanics here per the META-router discipline in `named-failure-modes.md:12-17,61-68`.
Historical provenance: Wave 14 sub-rule at this heading promoted after n=8 cumulative dogfood evidence.

## Within-cycle revision discipline (axis-9)

When a verdict-flip is proposed mid-synthesis (e.g., DEFER → APPROVE), decompose the original blocker into its constituent claims and verify EACH claim separately. A single fact-confirm doesn't authorize multi-claim flip. Especially when the authority-source (team-lead summary, primary-doc paragraph) appears to authorize the flip — they're often summarizing one layer, not all.

**How to apply**:
- Before flipping a verdict mid-cycle, list the constituent claims that originally supported the blocker.
- Demand evidence for EACH claim separately. Bullet them.
- A summary statement ("upstream confirms X") is one-layer evidence — verify the LAYER it summarizes is the layer the blocker depended on.
- If you flip, document the per-claim evidence trail in your synthesis.

## Spec self-review (design-time, single-source doc-only checklist — not convergence-gate ADOPT-NOW)

Reference: `Z:/repos/deps/superpowers/skills/brainstorming/SKILL.md:116-124 @ HEAD e7a2d16476bf042e9add4699c9d018a90f86e4a6 [VERIFIED 2026-05-02]` — single-source Superpowers checklist. Convergence-gate Axis 1 remains incomplete; this section is a bounded doc-only design-time spec-quality checklist that complements existing post-write git-diff verification, not a strict ADOPT-NOW claim.

Do not cite this section as convergence-backed ADOPT-NOW evidence; it is a local doc-quality lint pending Axis-1 convergence.

**When to apply**: immediately after writing any spec document (rule design, plan, ADR, codex T1 consult prompt, design-doc) and BEFORE the spec is consumed by reviewers / agents / codex T1. Apply with fresh eyes — re-read the document as if it were authored by someone else.

1. **Placeholder scan**: Any "TBD", "TODO", incomplete sections, or vague requirements? Fix them. (sss-local extension: also scan for `[UNKNOWN]`, `[INFERRED]`, `XXX`, `FIXME` markers; resolve load-bearing uncertainty, or make explicitly non-blocking before spec ship.)
2. **Internal consistency**: Do any sections contradict each other? Does the architecture match the feature descriptions? (sss-local: also check that frontmatter `paths:` glob matches actual cite locations in body.)
3. **Scope check**: Is this focused enough for a single implementation plan, or does it need decomposition? (sss-local: cross-ref `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md` cycle-300 ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE and `codification-threshold.md` bounded-ship ceiling.)
4. **Ambiguity check**: Could any requirement be interpreted two different ways? If so, pick one and make it explicit. (sss-local: aligns with `evidence-policy.md` `[VERIFIED]/[INFERRED]/[UNKNOWN]` markers — ambiguity often surfaces as a missing marker.)

Fix issues inline. No need to re-review — just fix and move on. Sister to:
- §Within-cycle revision discipline (axis-9) — covers verdict-flip discipline DURING verification rounds; this section covers spec-quality discipline BEFORE any verdict step
- §Reporting categories (OVER/UNDER/HONEST-NON-FINDING) — covers verification-output classification AFTER spec is consumed; this section covers spec-input quality BEFORE consumption

## Edge cases

- **Pure doc-memo writes by trusted teammates**: `git diff` is cheap (~1s) and catches missing frontmatter, partial renders, accidental indentation breakage. Run anyway — discipline is unconditional, not trust-tier gated.
- **File looks present but doesn't compile**: a common UNDER-report failure mode. A `Write` can land successfully (size > 0) yet content has a syntax error from partial render. ALWAYS pair `git status` (presence) with smoke-import (`python -c "import X"`) for new Python files.
- **Race conditions on background-spawned agents**: when Task notifications arrive mid-prompt, the next `git diff` probe might catch in-flight Edit/Write calls. Solution: check active TaskList before committing.
- **Intentional reverts shipped as the deliverable**: if teammate output IS a `git revert` or `git reset`, the diff direction inverts — verify the revert was the intended action, not an unintended rollback of prior work.
- **Infrastructure-coincident failures**: when a teammate fails during a known infrastructure event (proxy restart, service deploy, network blip), the `git diff` probe is THE primary signal because the agent's ship-report channel is exactly what the infrastructure event broke — DO NOT downgrade verification confidence just because the failure has a plausible non-agent cause.
- **Authority-source summary mistaken for verification**: when a teammate / primary-doc / report says "X confirmed", verify which LAYER they confirmed. Summary at L1 ≠ verification at L2-L5. Especially load-bearing when team-lead summary appears to authorize a verdict-flip.
- **Promotion-ship dogfood**: when promoting a feedback memory to a rule, the brief itself can carry the same drift the rule guards against. Critic should self-disclose unverified instance attributions; team-lead should spot-check brief cites against original cycle artifacts before applying.
- **Files outside the git tree** (e.g., `~/.codex/config.toml`, `Z:/repos/deps/<X>/` checkouts): the `git diff` check doesn't apply directly; substitute with `cp` backup before edit + post-edit Read-back to verify content matches intent.

## Jurisdiction

This is a **workflow-discipline** rule for the team-lead synthesis-pre-commit moment.

- DISTINCT from `.claude/rules/research-protocol.md` VERIFY corollary 1 (Pre-modification state + runtime probe) — that is the **executor-layer** existing-state check (proposer + critic verify state BEFORE proposing edits).
- DISTINCT from `.claude/rules/research-protocol.md` VERIFY corollary 2 (Audit producer-consumer coupling) — that is post-edit observability.
- This rule fills the gap between pre-edit (research-protocol) and post-edit observability (audit-infra): it covers the synthesis-pre-commit moment when team-lead aggregates teammate reports.
- Cite-vs-concept semantic-equivalence axis-2 (fire-location / payload-schema / consumer-contract): FORWARD-REF — not yet ported to claude-sota. Parent `Z:/claude/rules/research-protocol.md` §Cite-vs-concept cycle-465 is TIER-3 provenance only, not authority.

# TIER-3 local codification provenance only: this rule was promoted from `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Pre-apply Mia rule on agent ship-prescriptions` sub-section (Wave 14 codification commit `10d9455` 2026-05-04) to standalone rule after n=8 cumulative dogfood.

When an agent (architect / codex T1 prescription / sota-researcher / Explore / general-purpose / codex-rescue / gpt5-reviewer) returns a ship-prescription with `prescribed_edits` / specific file:line claims about gaps to close, **pre-apply the Mia rule (verify against runtime state) BEFORE writing any Edit**. The agent's prescription IS NOT TRUSTED until verified — even when conf ≥ 0.90 + Pattern A trigger.

The pattern: **AGENT reads brief context but DOES NOT probe runtime state → produces CATEGORY-CLAIM that LOOKS LIKE OPERATIONAL-CLAIM** (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator`). Agents systematically conflate "should be wired" with "is wired", "should be 80" with "is 80", "is unwired" with "is wired", without checking actual state via `git log` / `Grep` / `Glob`. This rule converts post-hoc OVER detection (existing `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` shape) into PRE-emptive verification (this rule's shape) BEFORE any Edit fires.

## When this rule applies

Trigger on **prescription shape, regardless of file class**. Apply to actionable prescription shapes:

- `prescribed_edits` listing specific file:line + content gaps
- gap-claim ("X is unwired" / "X is missing" / "X needs adding")
- quantitative claim ("70→80" / "12 files" / "5 hooks")
- status flip ("X is FORWARD-REF; promote to ACTIVE")

Do **NOT** apply to (avoid paying the discipline tax on non-actionable returns):

- Pure analysis returns (no `prescribed_edits` / no concrete file:line claim)
- Pure HONEST-NON-FINDING returns (agent already probed and found nothing)
- Pure read-only review output (gpt5-reviewer audit summary without specific edits)

## How to apply (BEFORE writing any Edit)

For each prescription:

1. **Decompose the gap-claim** into testable sub-claims (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Within-cycle revision discipline (axis-9)`)
2. **Pick the cheapest probe per sub-claim** (the §Subclaim-type discriminator informs probe-type selection):
   - File-existence claim → `Glob` or `ls -la`
   - Line-content claim → `Read <path>:<lines>` or `Grep`
   - Wire-status claim → `Grep <hook-name> .claude/settings.json`
   - Closed-loop arc claim → `git log --grep="<surface>" --oneline`
   - Count claim → actual `wc -l` / `Grep -c` not memory
   - SHAPE-CLAIM (settings.json hook entry / .mcp.json server / hook JSONL payload) → `Grep` + schema-validate per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Output-form verification modifier`
3. **Verify EACH sub-claim**. If ≥1 sub-claim refuted → CATEGORY-CLAIM drift detected
4. **Classify a refuted gap-prescription as OVER and drop it.** Reserve HONEST-NON-FINDING for an agent/operator report that explicitly probed an assumed target and found no such target (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` OVER vs HNF distinction). Do NOT silently apply the prescription; document the refutation in commit body
5. **Apply only the verified-surviving prescriptions** in the single atomic Pattern A fix-forward commit per `.claude/rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern A` — this rule is an admissibility filter BEFORE Pattern A's atomic-apply step (first drop OVER prescriptions, then apply all surviving verified prescriptions atomically)

## Empirical evidence ladder (n=29 cumulative — n=8 same-arc-codification-basis + n=21 cross-arc post-codification)

## Why this works

Agents are calibrated to find gaps; they're NOT calibrated to verify "is the gap still open?" before prescribing. Their reasoning surface is BRIEF + LOCAL-CONTEXT-WINDOW, not REPO-RUNTIME-STATE. The Mia-rule pre-apply discipline closes this gap by inserting a cheap (~1-3s) runtime probe between prescription-receipt and Edit-application.

ROI math: `Glob`/`Grep` probe ≈ 1-3s; reverting a wrong Edit ≈ 5-15min (revert + verify + arc memory). Cheap probe pays for itself ~100× per refuted prescription. At n=3/session base rate this session, savings = ~15-45min/session × N sessions/mo = compounding.

The rule is **bidirectional** — saves false-OVER ship cycles (rows 4, 6, 7) AND validates true-positive gap-claims (rows 5, 8). Pattern A admissibility filter shape: drop OVER, apply VERIFIED-GENUINE atomically.

## Anti-patterns

- **Trust agent prescription verbatim because conf ≥ 0.90** — refuted by n=3 same-arc evidence + n=5 cross-arc evidence. Codex T1 verdict at conf=0.92 still produced an OVER on Wave 13 prescription #2; architect at no-conf-stated produced 2 OVERs (Wave 12 S1 + S3); Wave 8 sota-researcher at no-conf-stated produced staleness drift on Ship #1 spec. Confidence does NOT correlate with prescription-truth on already-closed surfaces.
- **Skip cheap probe ("just N seconds")** — refuted by ROI math above (1-3s probe vs 5-15min revert; ~100× ROI per catch).
- **Cite the agent's verdict file as authority for the prescription** — per `cross-model-consensus.md §Source-cite discipline for consult prompts`: agent verdicts (`.claude/state/codex_consult_*_OUT.txt` + agent inline returns) are TIER-3 evidence trails, NOT TIER-1 sources. For local operational claims, the checked file/log/git history is the controlling operational evidence; the agent's prescription is a hypothesis pending probe-confirmation, not a verified fact.
- **Apply prescriptions in single atomic batch without per-prescription verification** — refuted by Wave 13 same-arc evidence: codex T1 prescription #2 was OVER in a 6-prescription batch; if I'd applied all 6 at once, the duplicate would have shipped. Per-prescription verification is necessary even when the agent batches.
- **Conflate OVER with HONEST-NON-FINDING** — these are distinct `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories`: OVER = report claims more than git shows (refuted prescription class); HNF = report says "I probed for X, found nothing" (agent did the probe). A refuted gap-prescription where I probed and found the gap closed = OVER, NOT HNF (the agent didn't probe; I did).
- **Apply Mia BEFORE codex T1 finishes** — Mia is a PRE-Edit gate; if codex T1 is still running, wait for verdict (Pattern A) OR honor Pattern B HNF disposition (timeout-without-verdict). DO NOT race Mia probe + Pattern A apply against an in-flight T1 — the T1 may correct or refute the Mia prescription itself (recursive: codex T1 ratifies Mia ratifies prescription).

## Sister-rule integration

- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` — post-hoc OVER detection on agent returns; this rule is the PRE-emptive specialization
- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` — UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY-CLAIM classification IS the type-system for the Mia-rule probe selection (CATEGORY → cheap-grep; OPERATIONAL → runtime-probe; UPSTREAM → re-WebFetch)
- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Output-form verification modifier (SHAPE-CLAIM)` — when the prescription targets a structured-output surface (settings.json hook entry / .mcp.json / hook JSONL), use the SHAPE-CLAIM probe path (compile/schema-validate) as the cheapest probe
- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Within-cycle revision discipline (axis-9)` — decompose-before-flip applies recursively to agent prescriptions
- `agent-harness-fit-verification.md §The 7 sub-classes Probe 4 plugin-namespace` — same Mia-rule shape applied to plugin-loaded namespace BEFORE adoption
- `cross-model-consensus.md §Verdict report shape (synthesis-layer-verify integration)` — OVER detection on codex output; this rule generalizes to all agent classes (architect / sota-researcher / Explore / etc.)
- `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern A` — this rule is an admissibility filter before Pattern A: first drop/refute OVER prescriptions, then apply all surviving verified prescriptions in the single atomic Pattern A fix-forward commit. The rule does NOT silently weaken Pattern A's atomic-apply requirement; it tightens the input set BEFORE Pattern A applies
- `codex-t1-auto-wedge-recovery.md` — sister Wave 16 fire-4 promotion (FM-14 dedicated rule); both rules use Pattern A admissibility filter shape

## Update triggers

Re-evaluate this rule when:
- A 4th distinct agent class produces an OVER claim (would expand the n=3 evidence ladder beyond architect + codex-T1 + sota-researcher)
- An agent class consistently produces ZERO OVER claims (e.g., gpt5-reviewer with explicit "verify against runtime state" mandate in initialPrompt) — would suggest the Mia-rule discipline is portable as agent-side prompt extension
- A `.claude/hooks/scripts/<hook>.py` ships that mechanically pre-applies this discipline (e.g., a PreToolUse:Edit gate that probes runtime state when prescriptions are detected in transcript) — flip from operator-discipline to mechanical-enforcement
- Agent's prescription correctly anticipates a NOT-YET-CLOSED surface that the operator's Mia-rule probe also confirms — promotes confidence in the agent class for future prescriptions on that surface
- A 3rd kind of "bidirectional" outcome emerges (beyond REFUTED-SKIP catch + GENUINE-gap-VALIDATE) — e.g., partial-OVER where some sub-claims hold and others don't; would require finer-grained prescription-decomposition discipline

When an agent (sota-researcher / architect / general-purpose / Explore / code-reviewer / debugger / codex T1) returns a ship-prescription containing claim X — OR when stale task-tracking text / prior memory snapshot / past-fire summary records claim X — and that claim becomes the source-of-truth for orchestrator-side synthesis → next fire's task brief → next agent's dispatch context, the wrong claim propagates 1-3 fires before runtime probe surfaces refutation. The recovery is decompose-by-sub-claim Mia probe at synthesis-vs-brief boundary, NOT only at apply boundary (which `mia-pre-apply.md` covers).

This rule is the **propagation-boundary peer to mia-pre-apply.md's apply-boundary discipline** — same shape (verify-before-trust) applied at different boundary points across multiple fires.

## The drift cascade structure

The drift cascade has 3 propagation hops:

```
[Agent A return / stale text] → Orchestrator synthesis → Task brief → Agent B dispatch → Mia probe at runtime → REFUTED
   (claim X)                       (verbatim cite)        (verbatim cite)   (assumes X)        (catches OVER)
```

Each hop preserves X verbatim because there's no cheap probe at the hop boundary; Mia is currently codified at the **apply boundary** (per `mia-pre-apply.md §How to apply`), not at every cite-propagation boundary. FM-20 codifies the synthesis-and-brief-composition boundaries explicitly.

## When this rule applies

ANY of these triggers fires this rule:

1. Agent return contains multi-clause prescription (3+ independent claims that could be sub-decomposed)
2. Orchestrator-side synthesis cites a prior agent verdict / memory file / task tracking text VERBATIM in next-fire brief
3. Next-fire brief carries claim X without explicit `[VERIFIED via <probe>]` marker per `cross-model-consensus.md §Evidence marker discipline` MUST-USE convention
4. Cross-fire propagation suspected: claim X originated 1-3 fires back; no runtime probe at any hop boundary

## How to apply (forward-only convention)

When an agent return contains multi-clause prescriptions:

1. **Decompose by sub-claim** — break the agent's "X+Y+Z" return into 3 independent claims; each gets its own probe shape per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` (UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY)
2. **Mia-probe each sub-claim INDEPENDENTLY at synthesis time** — do NOT cite X verbatim into the next-fire brief without runtime probe
3. **Cite the probe outcome verbatim in synthesis** — "Agent A claim X1: VERIFIED via `Grep`; X2: REFUTED via `Read file:line`; X3: PENDING-PROBE"
4. **Refuted sub-claims DROPPED from next-fire brief** — do not propagate
5. **At next-fire dispatch**: brief cites only verified sub-claims + explicit `[VERIFIED via <probe>]` markers per `cross-model-consensus.md §Evidence marker discipline` MUST-USE convention codified iter-107

## Recovery actions when FM-20 fires

When current-fire Mia probe surfaces propagated OVER from N-fires-back:

1. **DROP refuted sub-claim from current fire's apply scope**
2. **Document refutation in commit body** with `[VERIFIED via <probe>]` cite-trail
3. **Pivot ship to verified-surviving prescriptions** OR HONEST-NON-FINDING if all sub-claims refuted (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories`)
4. **Trace cite-propagation backward** through prior synthesis docs to find the originating agent claim (helps ladder-tracking)
5. **Forward-only correction** per `port-note-discipline.md §6` — do NOT rewrite historical commit bodies; correct the LIVE state and codify forward

## Anti-patterns

- **Cite agent claim verbatim into next-fire brief without sub-claim Mia probe** — refuted by n=5 cumulative cross-arc evidence. Agent confidence ≥0.85 does NOT correlate with sub-claim accuracy. Each sub-claim needs its own probe.
- **Synthesize first, probe later** — refuted by Wave 19 fire-22 evidence: orchestrator-side synthesis-Mia caught D2 + C OVER claims AFTER agent return but BEFORE fire-23 dispatch (3-OVER catch documented at commit `d2a765e` body). The probe-at-synthesis catches before propagation hop 2; without it, the OVER reaches next agent's dispatch context.
- **Treat task-tracking text as authoritative for source-of-truth claims** — refuted by Wave 37 AGENTS.md catch. Task tracking text (e.g., "GAP CONFIRMED ~170 LOC draft ready" in task #512) records state AT WRITE-TIME; subsequent ship-completion commits close gaps but task text doesn't auto-update. Per `evidence-policy.md` Marker Decay corollary.
- **Treat HONEST-NON-FINDING as agent failure** — per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` HNF distinction. An agent that probes its own claim and self-refutes (Agent A fire-22 ccusage self-catch; Agent A Wave 37 reverse-Mia AGENTS.md+context7) is the highest-quality return shape — preserve as evidence, do not re-spawn.
- **Skip the recursive-OVER probe at synthesis layer** — refuted by Wave 19 fire-22 close: synthesis-Mia caught 3 OVER claims that would have shipped if I'd cited Agent D2's "graphify NEW" verbatim into fire-23 brief. The probe budget is cheap (1-3s) vs revert cost (5-15 min).

## Sister-rule integration

- `.claude/rules/mia-pre-apply.md` — sibling discipline for **prescription-to-edit** boundary; FM-20 codifies the **claim-propagation-across-fires** boundary. The two are orthogonal: Mia at apply-time catches direct prescription drift; FM-20 catches cite-trail propagation drift across multiple synthesis hops. Wave 38+39 promotion chain matches mia-pre-apply.md's Wave 14 codification + Wave 16 fire-7 promotion shape.
- `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` — OVER classification applies recursively (agent OVER → orchestrator OVER → next-fire OVER until probe). FM-20 is the meta-pattern: this rule's OVER-detection should fire at every synthesis layer, not just at the team-lead-final-synthesis layer.
- `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` — UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY classification IS the type-system for FM-20 sub-claim decomposition (each sub-claim gets its own probe shape).
- `.claude/rules/named-failure-modes.md` FM-20 row — META-router; catalog row routes to here per Wave 38 commit `4b5d87b`. Wave 40 (next fire) flips MEMBER → OWNED-by status.
- `.claude/rules/cross-model-consensus.md §Verdict report shape (synthesis-layer-verify integration)` — FM-20 fits the OVER class within codex T1/T2/T3 verdicts; agent returns are TIER-3 evidence trails, NOT TIER-1 sources, per cardinal-rule-1 in CLAUDE.md L7 + cross-model-consensus.md §Source-cite discipline for consult prompts.
- `.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md` Marker Decay corollary — task-tracking text claims valid AT WRITE-TIME may decay to OVER at recall-time; FM-20's recovery action #5 cites this corollary explicitly.
- `.claude/rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern A` — single-fix-forward at NEEDS-REVISION conf 0.88-0.93; this rule's recovery action #1 (DROP refuted sub-claim) is admissibility-filter complement to Pattern A's atomic-apply.
- `.claude/rules/fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md` — sister Wave 34 promotion (FM-17 dedicated rule); same recursive-promotion-fire shape (Wave 38 catalog row + Wave 39 dedicated rule = 2 atomic units precedent at Wave 36+34).

## Promotion threshold (cycle-322 jurisdiction)

- **n=5 cumulative dogfood ladder**: Wave 17 fire-19 + Wave 19 fire-22 + Wave 19 fire-24 + Wave 37 AGENTS.md + Wave 37 context7 — well past n≥3 self-observed bar
- **n=4 same-arc 2026-05-05**: explicit update-trigger threshold from memory file:83 ("A 4th instance of FM-20 surfaces in same arc... would promote META-router to dedicated rule")
- **Expected savings ≥10 min/mo**: ~5-15 min revert-cycle per pre-apply OVER-catch × ~3-5 OVER catches/arc = ~25-75 min/arc savings; compounding across waves
- **LOC ≤200**: this rule ~120 LOC well under ceiling
- **TIER-1 cite chain acquired**: 2 TIER-1 SOTA cite-anchors (superpowers + Karpathy) + 7 TIER-2 sister-rule cites in header block
- **Recursive-promotion-fire dogfood**: matches 4 prior precedents (mia-pre-apply.md Wave 16 fire-7 / fm19-readonly-guard-sidestep.md Wave 17 D1 / advanced-agent-team-standing-directive.md Wave 24-D / fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md Wave 34) — same META-router → dedicated-rule shape

## Update triggers

Re-evaluate this rule when:
- A 6th cumulative instance lands — ladder advances to n=6; document in §Origin & promotion table
- A pattern emerges where Mia-at-synthesis catches the OVER but Mia-at-apply doesn't (would suggest synthesis-layer probes are higher-leverage; promote synthesis-layer probe to default discipline)
- A subagent class consistently produces zero OVER claims (would suggest the discipline has been operationalized at the agent layer; could simplify rule scope)
- Anthropic CC ships a per-claim provenance tracking primitive (e.g., agent return → claim → source-evidence chain) that surfaces sub-claim drift structurally — would obviate manual probe + flip rule from operator-discipline to gate-enforced
- A 3rd kind of "bidirectional" outcome emerges (beyond REFUTED-SKIP catch + GENUINE-gap-VALIDATE) — would require finer-grained prescription-decomposition discipline

## Codified Agent Action Record (AAR) schema (DOC-ONLY this fire)

Per W145-F1 Axis-4 #1 + Mia probe of existing capture, eee runtime emits **partial-AAR** per Claude Code session-tool-call interaction:

```yaml
agent_action_record:
  run_id: ${session_id}              # ✅ Phoenix span.session.id
  git_sha: ${HEAD at start_time}      # ✅ commit-on-stop chains capture HEAD pre+post
  git_sha_after: ${HEAD at end_time}  # ✅ commit-on-stop chains
  model_id: ${ANTHROPIC_MODEL}        # ✅ Phoenix openinference span.model
  prompt: ${user_prompt_full}         # ✅ OTEL_LOG_USER_PROMPTS=1 (PII note: local-only Phoenix)
  tool_calls: [...]                   # ✅ OTEL_LOG_TOOL_DETAILS=1
  tool_allowlist: ${.claude/settings.json hashed}  # ⚠️ static; not per-loop snapshot
  dep_lock: ${node_modules + .venv + .cargo}       # ❌ GAP — not per-loop snapshot
  test_command: ${last_test_invocation}            # ❌ GAP — no field
  token_cost: ${input + output + total + USD}      # ✅ cpa-usage-keeper :8079 JSON
  diff: ${git diff HEAD --stat / --shortstat}      # ✅ commit-on-stop / commit-on-stop-throttled
```

## Replay workflow (DOC-ONLY this fire)

To replay an autonomous-loop incident from existing captured surfaces:

1. **Identify incident time-range** from operator-facing wall-clock
2. **Phoenix UI** at `http://127.0.0.1:16006/` project=eee — filter spans by `session.id` or `start_time` range; retrieve prompt + tool_calls + model + duration
3. **cpa-usage-keeper :8079** dashboard — retrieve token/cost per account for time range
4. **Git history** — `git log --since=<start> --until=<end>` to find commits emitted by cwc commit-on-stop chains (LOC delta + diff)
5. **Session JSONL** at `${CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_DIR}/` (per CLAUDE.local.md ENV f) — transcript fallback for full Claude Code interaction record
6. **Manual reconstruct** dep-lock + test-command from git-tracked state (since those aren't auto-captured)
